| 简体中文 TypePHP A native AOT compiler for PHP Compile PHP source code into native machine code ahead of time — producing native executables, PHP extensions, and shared libraries — while keeping the PHP syntax you already know. What is TypePHP? TypePHP is an Ahead-Of-Time (AOT) compiler that translates PHP source code into C++ and then into native machine code. Unlike a bytecode cache or a VM, it does not interpret opcodes at runtime: it generates optimized native binaries that run directly on the CPU. It keeps familiar PHP syntax and adds compile-time type information, so the compiler can emit fast, statically-typed C++ for hot paths. Dynamic PHP values, internal functions, reflection, and object metadata continue to interoperate with the Zend runtime through PHPX; user functions are not executed as Zend opcodes after they have been compiled. TypePHP is written entirely in PHP and is fully self-hosting : the tpc compiler binary is built by compiling the compiler's own PHP source code with TypePHP. The bootstrap chain is pure PHP — no C or C++ glue in the compiler itself. TypePHP is under active development. It intentionally supports a defined, testable subset of PHP rather than claiming drop-in compatibility with every dynamic PHP program. Read Compatibility model and the incompatible-feature list before adopting it for an existing application. How it works PHP source + .stub.php declarations + optional C/C++ sources │ ▼ parse, validate, and collect declarations │ ▼ lower function bodies and constants to C++17 │ ▼ native compiler + reusable object/PCH caches │ ▼ executable | PHP extension | shared library | WASI component The prepare phase builds the complete symbol model without allocating runtime cache IDs. Constants and declaration defaults retain their AST until the convert phase, where they are lowered after all project symbols are known. This two-phase design keeps multi-file and self-hosted builds deterministic. Features Self-hosting, written in PHP — the TypePHP compiler is implemented entirely in PHP and bootstraps itself: tpc compiles the compiler's own source into a native binary. True AOT compilation — PHP is lowered to C++17, then to native machine code. No interpreter, no opcode cache, no JIT warm-up. Three native build modes — build a native bin executable, a loadable PHP ext extension, or a reusable lib shared library from the same codebase. Native type system — int , float , and bool map directly to C++ scalar types ( int64_t , double , bool ) for orders-of-magnitude speedups on numeric code. High-precision numerics — bigInt (GMP), decimal (libmpdec), and bigFloat (MPFR), with typed operators and method APIs. Strongly-typed containers — std::array , std::vector , std::map , and std::ordered_map with compile-time element types; up to 10× faster than PHP arrays and on par with C++ std::vector . Universal methods — call methods directly on primitives ( $s-&gt;upper() , $arr-&gt;contains() , $big-&gt;mul(2) ); statically-known calls are resolved directly at compile time. Mixed C++ / PHP — call C++ functions from PHP (and vice versa) for performance-critical kernels. Compile-time functions &amp; keywords — any() , refval() , objval() , expected() , unexpected() , plus toInt() , toString() , toArray() and friends. Compile-time safety — #[Immutable] read-only contracts and #[ArrayDef] array-shape metadata, checked at compile time with zero runtime cost. Compile-time code generation — #[Getter] , #[Setter] , #[With] , #[Constructor] , #[Printer] , and #[Arrayable] generate type-safe methods from property declarations. Modern PHP support — PHP 8.4 property hooks, asymmetric visibility, PHP 8.5 clone() -with, and (void) discard expressions. Cross-platform &amp; WASM — Linux, Windows, and macOS targets for x64 and ARM64, plus WASI 0.2 and browser (Jco) output. Python bridge — generate IDE helpers for Python modules and convert Python scripts to TypePHP. Why TypePHP? TypePHP AOT Opcode cache (OPcache) JIT (PHP 8+) Compilation target Native machine code Bytecode Machine code (trace) Startup / warm-up None (already compiled) Per-process warm-up JIT warm-up Type-driven optimization Compile-time, full-program None Limited, trace-based Native executable output Yes No No Source code protection Compiled to machine code Bytecode (reversible) Bytecode (reversible) Deterministic performance Yes No No Strengths over plain PHP: Near-native performance. Numeric and container-heavy hot paths compile down to the same machine code a C++ program would produce. See the benchmark below. Source protection. Your source is compiled away — shipped artifacts are native binaries, not readable PHP files. Native process entry. Binary mode starts directly from a native executable and does not require the PHP CLI or a separate interpreter process. The executable still embeds/links PHPX, libphp , and any configured native libraries, which must be available in the deployment package. Gradual typing that actually pays off. Add use native_types , std:: containers, and type declarations only where performance matters; the rest stays ordinary PHP. Zend ecosystem interop. Extension mode loads as a standard PHP extension, and projects can call supported internal functions and require other Zend extensions explicitly. Requirements PHP 8.4 – 8.5 CLI, development headers, and php-config The matching PHP embed library ( libphp.so or libphp.dylib ) for binary/shared-library builds on Unix-like systems GCC 9+ (or Clang) with C++17 CMake 3.24+ Composer 2 High-precision math libraries: GMP , MPFR (libmpdec is bundled with PHPX) # Ubuntu/Debian sudo apt install build-essential cmake pkg-config libgmp-dev libmpfr-dev # RHEL/CentOS/Fedora sudo dnf install gcc gcc-c++ cmake pkgconf-pkg-config gmp-devel mpfr-devel # Arch Linux sudo pacman -S base-devel cmake pkgconf gmp mpfr GMP powers bigInt and MPFR powers bigFloat . The decimal type is backed by libmpdec, which is bundled with PHPX — no separate install required. Linux x64 is the primary development and full-test CI platform. The compiler also has Windows, macOS, ARM64, and WASI backends; availability of PHP embed, toolchain, and third-party libraries still determines which target can be built on a given host. Native release assets are built with the latest PHP 8.5 ZTS release. TypePHP publishes Linux x64, Linux ARM64, macOS ARM64, and Windows x64 packages. Native NTS and 32-bit x86 packages are not provided. Linux and macOS archives contain the compiler and production Composer dependencies, while the Windows archive contains the complete matching PHP/PHPX runtime and SDK. Installation Via Composer composer require --dev swoole/typephp Then compile your project: vendor/bin/tpc.php project.yml When working inside the TypePHP source repository, use the local entry point instead: bin/tpc.php project.yml From source git clone https://github.com/swoole/typephp.git cd typephp composer install php bin/tpc.php --help PHPX_HOME may point to a separate PHPX checkout or installation. PHP_HOME may point to the PHP embed prefix; it must contain bin/php-config , PHP headers, and lib/libphp.so on Unix-like systems. Building libphp.so Binary and shared-library builds require PHP's embed SAPI. If libphp.so is missing on Linux, tpc.php can interactively download the PHP source and build it for you. A PHP extension build resolves Zend symbols from the host SAPI and must not load a second libphp . See Automatic libphp.so build . Quick Start Create hello.php : &lt;?php function main (): void { echo " Hello World! \n" ; var_dump ( PHP_VERSION ); var_dump ( php_uname ()); } Compile and run it: bin/tpc.php hello.php ./hello Example output (the exact PHP version and platform strings depend on the linked runtime): Hello World! string(5) "8.x.x" string(16) "Linux ..." Binary mode requires a global main() function. It may be declared with no parameters, or as main(int $argc, array $argv) to receive command-line arguments, and must return void . Top-level executable statements are not allowed; executable code belongs in a function or method. Compilation Modes TypePHP supports three build modes, selected with -m / --mode : Mode Flag Output Needs main() Typical use Binary -m bin (default) Executable Yes CLI tools, long-running services, standalone apps Extension -m ext PHP .so / .dll No Loading compiled functions/classes into a PHP SAPI Library -m lib Shared library plus generated .stub.php No Reusing a compiled TypePHP API from another project # Binary (default) bin/tpc.php app.php -o myapp # PHP extension bin/tpc.php extension/ -m ext -o my_extension # Shared library; also generates mylib.stub.php bin/tpc.php lib/ -m lib -o mylib See Compilation modes for details. Project configuration For multi-file projects, keep repeatable build settings in project.yml : name : myapp mode : bin php-version : " 8.5 " optimize : 2 job : 8 build-dir : build cxx-std : c++17 sources : - src - cpp-src - path : src/php85 if : PHP_VERSION_ID &gt;= 80500 - path : src/windows if : PHP_OS_FAMILY == "Windows" ignore : - src/experimental include-paths : - native/include defines : - FEATURE_FAST_PATH=1 link-paths : - native/lib link-libs : - curl # Zend extension requirements, not native linker libraries. # `extension-dependencies` is the equivalent long name; do not use both. ext-deps : - pdo_mysql - curl Paths are resolved relative to the YAML file. A source entry may be a file or directory; conditional entries support PHP_VERSION , PHP_VERSION_ID , and PHP_OS_FAMILY . CLI arguments override their YAML counterparts. Native linker dependencies belong in link-libs ; ext-deps writes ZEND_MOD_REQUIRED entries so Zend can reject loading when a required PHP extension is missing. The build directory contains generated C++, dependency objects, and the precompiled-header cache. Reusing it makes incremental builds much faster; use --force only when the reusable PHPX objects must be rebuilt. See Compiler CLI for all project keys and command-line precedence rules. Compatibility model TypePHP follows PHP syntax and runtime behavior where they are compatible with ahead-of-time compilation, but it also makes several deliberate restrictions: global scope is declaration-only; executable statements must be inside a function or method; binary mode has a strict main() signature; use native_types opts scalar declarations into fixed native storage, so a value cannot later change to an incompatible type; statically-known calls and properties are compiled directly, while supported dynamic operations use PHPX/Zend runtime fallbacks; .stub.php files declare C++ or imported-library APIs and must contain empty bodies; #[Native] classes are not permitted in stub files; some highly dynamic reference, declaration, closure, and reflection patterns remain intentionally unsupported. The compatibility boundary is part of the public contract and has both positive and negative tests. Consult Incompatible PHP features for the current, specific list instead of assuming that absence from this README means support. Compile-time attributes and code generation TypePHP consumes its built-in code-generation attributes while lowering the class. The generated methods retain the declared property types and take part in the same conflict, inheritance, and final-method checks as explicitly declared methods. Attribute Target Generated API #[Getter] Instance property, including a promoted property public function getName(): T #[Setter] Mutable instance property, including a promoted property public function setName(T $name): void #[With] Mutable instance property, including a promoted property public function withName(T $name): static ; clones the object, updates the clone, and returns it #[Constructor] Declared instance property Adds the property to a generated public __construct() #[Printer] Named class public function __toString(): string #[Arrayable] Named class public function toArray(): array &lt;?php #[Printer(fields: [ ' id ' , ' name ' ])] #[Arrayable(fields: [ ' id ' , ' name ' ])] final class User { #[Constructor, Getter, With] public int $ id ; #[Constructor, Getter, Setter] public string $ name = ' guest ' ; } function main (): void { $ user = new User ( 7 ); $ user -&gt; setName ( ' Alice ' ); $ copy = $ user -&gt; withId ( 8 ); echo $ user -&gt; getId (); // 7 echo $ copy -&gt; getId (); // 8 echo $ user ; // User(id=7, name=Alice) echo $ user -&gt; toArray ()[ ' name ' ]; } Without fields , #[Printer] and #[Arrayable] use the class's own public instance properties. The positional form, such as #[Arrayable(['id'])] , is equivalent to #[Arrayable(fields: ['id'])] . #[Getter] , #[Setter] , and #[With] cannot target static properties or properties with hooks. #[Setter] and #[With] additionally reject readonly properties. #[Constructor] cannot be used when the class already declares __construct() , and required constructor properties must precede properties with defaults. A generated method name that conflicts with a declared or inherited final method is a compile-time error. Examples 1. Native types — compile-time numeric speedup &lt;?php use native_types ; function fib ( int $ n ): int { if ( $ n == 1 || $ n == 2 ) { return 1 ; } return fib ( $ n - 1 ) + fib ( $ n - 2 ); } function main ( int $ argc , array $ argv ): void { $ n = ( int ) $ argv [ 1 ]; $ begin = microtime ( true ); echo fib ( $ n ) . "\n" ; echo " Time: " . ( microtime ( true ) - $ begin ) . "\n" ; } bin/tpc.php fib.php -O3 -o fib ./fib 30 With use native_types , int variables become C++ int64_t and arithmetic compiles to plain CPU instructions instead of ZendVM calls. 2. High-precision numerics &lt;?php declare (strict_types= 1 ); use native_types ; function main (): void { // 54-digit integer — automatically detected and stored as bigInt $ a = std:: bigInt ( " 123456789012345678901234567890123456789012345678901234 " ); $ b = std:: bigInt ( " 987654321098765432109876543210987654321098765432109876 " ); echo $ a -&gt; add ( $ b )-&gt; toString () . "\n" ; // exact, no overflow // Exact decimal arithmetic — no binary floating-point error $ c = std:: decimal ( " 0.1 " )-&gt; add (std:: decimal ( " 0.2 " )); echo $ c -&gt; toString () . "\n" ; // "0.3" // 256-bit floating point $ pi = std:: bigFloat ( " 3.14159265358979323846264338327950288419716939937510 " ); echo $ pi -&gt; mul ( 2 )-&gt; toString () . "\n" ; } See High-precision types and Native types . 3. Strongly-typed containers &lt;?php use native_types ; function main (): void { $ vector = std:: vector (Type::Int); $ vector [] = 1 ; $ vector [] = 2 ; $ vector [] = 3 ; $ sum = 0 ; foreach ( $ vector as $ value ) { $ sum += $ value ; } echo $ sum . "\n" ; // 6 echo $ vector [ 1 ] . "\n" ; // 2 // key-value map with fixed key/value types $ map = std:: ordered_map (Type::String, Type::Int); $ map [ " a " ] = 1 ; $ map [ " b " ] = 2 ; } See Std containers . 4. Universal methods &lt;?php function main (): void { $ s = " hello world " ; echo $ s -&gt; length () . "\n" ; // strlen() echo $ s -&gt; upper () . "\n" ; // strtoupper() echo $ s -&gt; substr ( 0 , 5 ) . "\n" ; // substr() $ arr = [ 1 , 3 , 5 , 7 , 9 ]; echo $ arr -&gt; count () . "\n" ; // count() var_dump ( $ arr -&gt; contains ( 3 )); // in_array() $ big = std:: bigInt ( " 12345678901234567890 " ); echo $ big -&gt; mul ( 2 )-&gt; toString () . "\n" ; } Method calls on primitives are resolved at compile time into direct C/C++ function calls — no vtable lookup, no reflection, no runtime dispatch. See Universal methods . 5. Mixed C++ / PHP Write performance-critical kernels in C++ and call them from PHP: // math.cpp # include &lt; phpx.h &gt; using namespace php ; Int php_fast_sum (Int a, Int b) { return a + b; } &lt;?php // math.stub.php — declares the C++ function signature function fast_sum ( int $ a , int $ b ): int {} &lt;?php function main (): void { echo fast_sum ( 3 , 4 ) . "\n" ; // 7 } Add math.cpp , math.stub.php , and the calling PHP source to the same project configuration. The php_ C++ symbol prefix is the TypePHP callable ABI; stub functions provide type metadata only and must not contain an implementation. See Mixed C++/PHP . Benchmark PHP language benchmarks (from php-src) TypePHP runs the official bench.php and micro_bench.php language benchmarks that ship with the PHP source tree, compiled with -O3 : Benchmark Interpreted PHP TypePHP AOT ( -O3 ) Speedup bench.php (total) 5.034 s 0.603 s ~8× micro_bench.php (total) 13.045 s 2.021 s ~6.5× Both benchmarks measure core PHP language performance — function calls, object property access, array/hash access, string handling, control flow, and more. The checked-in workloads are benchmark/bench.php and benchmark/micro_bench.php . Additional focused performance regressions live in the same benchmark/ directory. These numbers are a project measurement snapshot, not a performance guarantee. PHP version, compiler, CPU, optimization flags, and enabled extensions can all change the result; compare on the same machine with the same workload before making deployment decisions. std::array vs PHP array A 10000×100000 element update loop, comparing PHP arrays against TypePHP's std::array and native C++: Implementation Time PHP array (JIT) 67.6 s std::array (TypePHP AOT) 6.4 s C++ std::vector 6.2 s std::array is roughly 10× faster than PHP arrays and performs close to the hand-written C++ result in this workload. See the benchmark in Std containers . Command Line bin/tpc.php &lt; file | dir | project.yml &gt; [options] [-- program-args...] Common usage: # Compile a single file bin/tpc.php app.php # Optimize and run, passing args to the program after `--` bin/tpc.php app.php -O3 -r -- --flag value # Compile a project defined in project.yml bin/tpc.php project.yml -O2 -j 8 # Build a PHP extension bin/tpc.php extension/ -m ext -o my_extension # Only generate C++ (skip compile &amp; link) bin/tpc.php app.php --dry --build-dir /tmp/typephp-build # Compile to WASI 0.2 bin/tpc.php --wasm app.php # Compile for the browser (requires jco) bin/tpc.php --wasm=browser app.php Key options: Option Description -O &lt;0-3&gt; Optimization level (default 0 ) -d , --debug Debug build with symbols and source tracking -o , --output &lt;file&gt; Output file name -m , --mode &lt;bin|lib|ext&gt; Build mode (default bin ) -r , --run Run after a successful build -j , --job &lt;num&gt; Parallel compile jobs (default 4 ) -f , --force Rebuild reusable PHPX objects instead of using the cache --build-dir &lt;dir&gt; Directory for generated C++ and intermediates --dry Generate C++ only, skip compile and link --php-version &lt;8.4|8.5&gt; PHP syntax version to accept --cxx-std &lt;ver&gt; C++ standard (e.g. c++17 , c++20 ) --march &lt;arch&gt; Target instruction set (e.g. native ) --target-platform &lt;triple&gt; Cross-compilation target triple --lto Enable link-time optimization --sanitize &lt;type&gt; Enable a sanitizer (e.g. address ) --profile Enable Linux gperftools profiling --format Format generated C++ with clang-format --no-literal-strings Disable the literal-string table optimization --no-progress , --no-color CI-friendly output controls -I , -D , -L , -l Repeatable native include, define, library path, and library options Run bin/tpc.php --help for the authoritative, up-to-date list. See Compiler CLI for details, including Bash completion: source &lt;( ./tpc --generate-completion=bash ) Troubleshooting libphp.so / libphp.dylib is missing: install/build the matching PHP embed SAPI, set PHP_HOME , or let bin/tpc.php offer the interactive Linux installer. PHPX cannot be found: set PHPX_HOME to a PHPX installation containing include/ and lib/libphpx.so (or the platform equivalent), then build PHPX before compiling the project. Startup crashes or ABI errors: the PHP headers, php-config , libphp , and loaded extension ABI must agree on the PHP version and ZTS/NTS mode. Do not mix artifacts from different PHP builds. Incremental builds are unexpectedly slow: keep a stable --build-dir so object and PCH caches can be reused. When an external test runner already runs several tests concurrently, avoid multiplying that concurrency by an unnecessarily large tpc -j value. A project compiles with bin/tpc.php but fails with tpc : reproduce with the self-hosted compiler. Bootstrap execution can expose dynamic-call or ABI paths that the PHP-hosted compiler does not exercise. Python bridge TypePHP ships a Python tool submodule that shares the tpc entry point: # Generate IDE helpers for Python modules ./tpc --gen-python-helper math ./tpc --gen-python-helper numpy --output-dir .ide-helper # Convert a Python script to TypePHP ./tpc --convert-python-to-php script.py &gt; script.php See Python tool submodule . Development and testing Install development dependencies and run the compiler unit suite: composer install PHPX_HOME=/path/to/phpx vendor/bin/phpunit PHPT is the end-to-end suite. Build the self-hosted compiler first and pass it explicitly to the test runner; using the Zend PHP executable as --compiler does not test the deployed compiler: PHPX_HOME=/path/to/phpx php bin/tpc.php project.yml --job 2 --no-progress php run-tests.php -q -j8 --compiler ./tpc tests/compiler Static analysis and the source-derived coverage matrix are separate checks: composer analyse php bin/analyze-test-coverage.php php bin/analyze-test-coverage.php \ --format=markdown --output=build/test-coverage.md --strict The coverage tool reports PHP version × feature × positive compilation × runtime semantics × negative diagnostics, plus concrete PHP-parser AST nodes. It intentionally does not publish a single percentage without an explicit denominator. See Test coverage analyzer . GitHub Actions runs PHPUnit and self-hosted PHPT on PHP 8.4 and 8.5. Changes to compiler behavior should add a focused PHPUnit test for internal/code-generation rules and a PHPT whenever runtime output or diagnostics are observable. Documentation Quick Start — minimal compilation flow Compilation modes — bin , ext , lib Compiler CLI — CLI arguments and project config Incompatible PHP features — current limits Native types — native scalar types High-precision types — BigInt / Decimal / BigFloat Std containers — strongly-typed containers Universal methods — compile-time method resolution Compile-time functions — any() , refval() , objval() , … Mixed C++/PHP — C++/PHP interop #[Immutable] — compile-time read-only contracts #[ArrayDef] — typed array-property contracts Property hooks — PHP 8.4 hook lowering and runtime metadata Object storage models — Zend object, Box, and Native class boundaries Generators — generator lowering and lifecycle Test coverage analyzer — AST and feature evidence matrix WASI build — WASI targets License TypePHP is licensed under the GNU General Public License v3.0 . 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eGhidra is a software reverse engineering (SRE) framework created and maintained by the\n\u003ca href=\"https://www.nsa.gov\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eNational Security Agency\u003c/a\u003e Research Directorate. This framework includes a suite of\nfull-featured, high-end software analysis tools that enable users to analyze compiled code on a\nvariety of platforms including Windows, macOS, and Linux. Capabilities include disassembly,\nassembly, decompilation, graphing, and scripting, along with hundreds of other features. Ghidra\nsupports a wide variety of processor instruction sets and executable formats and can be run in both\nuser-interactive and automated modes. Users may also develop their own Ghidra extension components\nand/or scripts using Java or Python.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eIn support of NSA's Cybersecurity mission, Ghidra was built to solve scaling and teaming problems\non complex SRE efforts, and to provide a customizable and extensible SRE research platform. NSA has\napplied Ghidra SRE capabilities to a variety of problems that involve analyzing malicious code and\ngenerating deep insights for SRE analysts who seek a better understanding of potential\nvulnerabilities in networks and systems.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eIf you are a U.S. citizen interested in projects like this, to develop Ghidra and other\ncybersecurity tools for NSA to help protect our nation and its allies, consider applying for a\n\u003ca href=\"https://www.intelligencecareers.gov/nsa\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003ecareer with us\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eSecurity Warning\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-security-warning\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Security Warning\" href=\"#security-warning\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eWARNING:\u003c/strong\u003e There are known security vulnerabilities within certain versions of Ghidra. the files named \"Source Code\" is not correct for this step.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eExtract the Ghidra release file\n\u003cul \u003cem\u003eGhidraDev\u003c/em\u003e plugin\nfor Eclipse. The plugin and its corresponding instructions can be found within a Ghidra release at\n\u003ccode\u003eExtensions/Eclipse/GhidraDev/\u003c/code\u003e or at \u003ca href=\"/NationalSecurityAgency/ghidra/blob/master/GhidraBuild/EclipsePlugins/GhidraDev/GhidraDevPlugin/README.md\"\u003ethis link\u003c/a\u003e. Alternatively, Visual Studio Code may\nbe used to edit scripts by clicking the Visual Studio Code icon in the Script Manager.\nFully-featured Visual Studio Code projects can be created from a Ghidra CodeBrowser window at\n\u003cem\u003eTools -\u0026gt; Create VSCode Module project\u003c/em\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eNOTE:\u003c/strong\u003e Both the \u003cem\u003eGhidraDev\u003c/em\u003e plugin for Eclipse and Visual Studio Code integrations only support\ndeveloping against fully built Ghidra installations which can be downloaded from the\n\u003ca href=\"https://github.com/NationalSecurityAgency/ghidra/releases\"\u003eReleases\u003c/a\u003e page.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 navigation README Code of conduct Contributing Apache-2.0 license Security More items Ghidra Software Reverse Engineering Framework Ghidra is a software reverse engineering (SRE) framework created and maintained by the National Security Agency Research Directorate. This framework includes a suite of full-featured, high-end software analysis tools that enable users to analyze compiled code on a variety of platforms including Windows, macOS, and Linux. Capabilities include disassembly, assembly, decompilation, graphing, and scripting, along with hundreds of other features. Ghidra supports a wide variety of processor instruction sets and executable formats and can be run in both user-interactive and automated modes. Users may also develop their own Ghidra extension components and/or scripts using Java or Python. In support of NSA's Cybersecurity mission, Ghidra was built to solve scaling and teaming problems on complex SRE efforts, and to provide a customizable and extensible SRE research platform. NSA has applied Ghidra SRE capabilities to a variety of problems that involve analyzing malicious code and generating deep insights for SRE analysts who seek a better understanding of potential vulnerabilities in networks and systems. If you are a U.S. citizen interested in projects like this, to develop Ghidra and other cybersecurity tools for NSA to help protect our nation and its allies, consider applying for a career with us . Security Warning WARNING: There are known security vulnerabilities within certain versions of Ghidra. Before proceeding, please read through Ghidra's Security Advisories for a better understanding of how you might be impacted. Install To install an official pre-built multi-platform Ghidra release: Install JDK 21 64-bit Download a Ghidra release file NOTE: The official multi-platform release file is named ghidra_&lt;version&gt;_&lt;release&gt;_&lt;date&gt;.zip which can be found under the "Assets" drop-down. Downloading either of the files named "Source Code" is not correct for this step. Extract the Ghidra release file NOTE: Do not extract on top of an existing installation Launch Ghidra: ./ghidraRun ( ghidraRun.bat for Windows) or launch PyGhidra : ./support/pyghidraRun ( support\pyghidraRun.bat for Windows) For additional information and troubleshooting tips about installing and running a Ghidra release, please refer to the Getting Started document which can be found at the root of a Ghidra installation directory. Build To create the latest development build for your platform from this source repository: Install build tools: JDK 25 64-bit Gradle 9.1.0+ (or provided Gradle wrapper if Internet connection is available) Python3 (version 3.9 to 3.14) with bundled pip GCC or Clang, and make (Linux/macOS-only) Microsoft Visual Studio 2017+ or Microsoft C++ Build Tools with the following components installed (Windows-only): MSVC Windows SDK C++ ATL Download and extract the source: Download from GitHub unzip ghidra-master cd ghidra-master NOTE: Instead of downloading the compressed source, you may instead want to clone the GitHub repository: git clone https://github.com/NationalSecurityAgency/ghidra.git Download additional build dependencies into source repository: NOTE: If an Internet connection is available and you did not install Gradle, the ./gradlew (or gradlew.bat ) command may be used in place of the gradle command in the following instructions. gradle -I gradle/support/fetchDependencies.gradle Create development build: gradle buildGhidra The compressed development build will be located at build/dist/ . For more detailed information on building Ghidra, please read the Developer's Guide . For issues building, please check the Known Issues section for possible solutions. Develop User Scripts and Extensions Ghidra installations support users writing custom scripts and extensions via the GhidraDev plugin for Eclipse. The plugin and its corresponding instructions can be found within a Ghidra release at Extensions/Eclipse/GhidraDev/ or at this link . Alternatively, Visual Studio Code may be used to edit scripts by clicking the Visual Studio Code icon in the Script Manager. Fully-featured Visual Studio Code projects can be created from a Ghidra CodeBrowser window at Tools -&gt; Create VSCode Module project . NOTE: Both the GhidraDev plugin for Eclipse and Visual Studio Code integrations only support developing against fully built Ghidra installations which can be downloaded from the Releases page. Advanced Development To develop the Ghidra tool itself, it is highly recommended to use Eclipse, which the Ghidra development process has been highly customized for. Install build and development tools: Follow the above build instructions so the build completes without errors Install Eclipse IDE for Java Developers Prepare the development environment: gradle prepdev eclipse buildNatives Import Ghidra projects into Eclipse: File -&gt; Import... General | Existing Projects into Workspace Select root directory to be your downloaded or cloned ghidra source repository Check Search for nested projects Click Finish When Eclipse finishes building the projects, Ghidra can be launched and debugged with the provided Ghidra Eclipse run configuration . For more detailed information on developing Ghidra, please read the Developer's Guide . Contribute If you would like to contribute bug fixes, improvements, and new features back to Ghidra, please take a look at our Contributor's Guide to see how you can participate in this open source project. max-height: 176px;\"\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\n\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp align=\"center\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cem\u003e\"Tailscale without Tailscale, by Tailscale\"\u003c/em\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch1 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eTailcat\u003c/h1\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-tailcat\" class=\"anchor\" 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eTailcat is a remix of Tailscale open source pieces to act like\n\u003ca href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netcat\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003enetcat\u003c/a\u003e, but over Tailscale's data plane,\nwithout Tailscale's control plane. Tailscale's data plane (\u003ccode\u003emagicsock\u003c/code\u003e,\ninternally) gives you point-to-point WireGuard®-encrypted tunnels between two\nmachines with DERP as the NAT-hole-punching communication side channel and the\nultimate relay-of-last-resort if NAT traversal fails. Instead of using the\nTailscale control plane, all \u003ccode\u003etailcat\u003c/code\u003e connection metadata is exchanged out of\nband, however you want.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe \u003ccode\u003etailcat\u003c/code\u003e CLI (in \u003ccode\u003ecmd/tailcat\u003c/code\u003e) is built on the \u003ccode\u003etailcat\u003c/code\u003e Go library\n(importable as \u003ca href=\"https://pkg.go.dev/github.com/tailscale/tailcat\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003egithub.com/tailscale/tailcat\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/a\u003e).\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eWhether you use \u003ccode\u003etailcat\u003c/code\u003e as a CLI tool or library, one side runs a \u003ccode\u003etailcat\u003c/code\u003e\nserver (listener) and gets back a short connection token. The other side passes\nthat token to \u003ccode\u003etailcat\u003c/code\u003e's client side to connect. All traffic between the two is\nencrypted end-to-end with WireGuard. The initial connection bootstraps through\na DERP server (\u003ca href=\"#bring-your-own-derp-relay\"\u003esee below\u003c/a\u003e), and then magicsock performs NAT traversal to\nupgrade to a direct peer-to-peer UDP connection when possible (usually!).\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eYou don't need a Tailscale account, root/admin access on the machine\n(it doesn't alter your machine's routing tables, DNS, etc.). It's just\na userspace library and CLI tool.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eAnd it's all open source.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eYou can use our free rate-limited DERP relays (the default DERP map is\n\u003ca href=\"https://tailcat.dev/derpmap.json\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003ehttps://tailcat.dev/derpmap.json\u003c/a\u003e) or you can \u003ca href=\"https://github.com/tailscale/tailscale/tree/main/cmd/derper#derp\"\u003erun your own\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThere's also an experimental in-browser web demo (tailcat compiled to\nWebAssembly) at \u003ca href=\"https://tailscale.github.io/tailcat/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003ehttps://tailscale.github.io/tailcat/\u003c/a\u003e that can send and\nreceive files or text, interoperating with the CLI. Browser traffic is\nrelayed over DERP only, with no direct connections until WebRTC\nsupport (\u003ca href=\"https://github.com/tailscale/tailcat/issues/4\" data-hovercard-type=\"issue\" data-hovercard-url=\"/tailscale/tailcat/issues/4/hovercard\"\u003e#4\u003c/a\u003e).\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eInstall\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-install\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Install\" href=\"#install\"\u003e\u003csvg 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"$ go install github.com/tailscale/tailcat/cmd/tailcat@latest\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003e$ go install github.com/tailscale/tailcat/cmd/tailcat@latest\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eOr with Nix flakes, run it directly or install it:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"$ nix run github:tailscale/tailcat\n$ nix profile install github:tailscale/tailcat\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003e$ nix run github:tailscale/tailcat\n$ nix profile install github:tailscale/tailcat\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eUsage\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-usage\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Usage\" href=\"#usage\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003ePipe stdin/stdout between two machines\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-pipe-stdinstdout-between-two-machines\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Pipe stdin/stdout between two machines\" href=\"#pipe-stdinstdout-between-two-machines\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eServer starts, printing out its ephemeral address:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"$ tailcat\n# Selected bootstrap relay region 302, San Francisco\n# 🐈 Server listening with new address: tcomFwWCCcjS5nKNqAod034nWoJZW0LZqDhhC8U_dKdnDRYQ8uNGFpGQEu\n(hangs, waiting...)\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003e$ tailcat\n\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e#\u003c/span\u003e Selected bootstrap relay region 302, San Francisco\u003c/span\u003e\n\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e#\u003c/span\u003e 🐈 Server listening with new address: tcomFwWCCcjS5nKNqAod034nWoJZW0LZqDhhC8U_dKdnDRYQ8uNGFpGQEu\u003c/span\u003e\n(hangs, waiting...)\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eAnd then the client can:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"$ echo hello | tailcat tcomFwWCCcjS5nKNqAod034nWoJZW0LZqDhhC8U_dKdnDRYQ8uNGFpGQEu\n$ \"\u003e\u003cpre\u003e$ \u003cspan class=\"pl-c1\"\u003eecho\u003c/span\u003e hello \u003cspan class=\"pl-k\"\u003e|\u003c/span\u003e tailcat tcomFwWCCcjS5nKNqAod034nWoJZW0LZqDhhC8U_dKdnDRYQ8uNGFpGQEu\n$ \u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThen the server unblocks:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"$ tailcat\n# Selected bootstrap relay region 302, San Francisco\n# 🐈 Server listening with new address: tcomFwWCCcjS5nKNqAod034nWoJZW0LZqDhhC8U_dKdnDRYQ8uNGFpGQEu\nhello\n$\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003e$ tailcat\n\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e#\u003c/span\u003e Selected bootstrap relay region 302, San Francisco\u003c/span\u003e\n\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e#\u003c/span\u003e 🐈 Server listening with new address: tcomFwWCCcjS5nKNqAod034nWoJZW0LZqDhhC8U_dKdnDRYQ8uNGFpGQEu\u003c/span\u003e\nhello\n$\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eExpose local ports through the tunnel\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-expose-local-ports-through-the-tunnel\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Expose local ports through the tunnel\" href=\"#expose-local-ports-through-the-tunnel\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eOr you can serve a local TCP port, forwarded to localhost:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"$ tailcat --serve=8080,8443 # or --serve=all\n# 🐈 Server listening with new address: tcXXXXXXXXX\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003e$ tailcat --serve=8080,8443 \u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e#\u003c/span\u003e or --serve=all\u003c/span\u003e\n\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e#\u003c/span\u003e 🐈 Server listening with new address: tcXXXXXXXXX\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eAnd then the client:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"$ tailcat tcXXXXXXXXX 8080\nGET / HTTP/1.1\nHost: foo\n\nHTTP/1.1 200 OK\n....\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003e$ tailcat tcXXXXXXXXX 8080\nGET / HTTP/1.1\nHost: foo\n\nHTTP/1.1 200 OK\n....\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eAuth-free SSH server\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-auth-free-ssh-server\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Auth-free SSH server\" href=\"#auth-free-ssh-server\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eOn Linux and macOS, you can run an SSH server too with no auth. (If you want auth, you can just \u003ccode\u003etailcat --serve=22\u003c/code\u003e and proxy to your system SSH server)\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"$ tailcat --serve=no-auth-ssh\n# 🐈 Server listening with new address: tcXXXXXXXXX\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003e$ tailcat --serve=no-auth-ssh\n\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e#\u003c/span\u003e 🐈 Server listening with new address: tcXXXXXXXXX\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eAnd on the client side:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"$ tailcat ssh tcXXXXXXXXX\n$ tailcat ssh tcXXXXXXXXX ls -la\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003e$ tailcat ssh tcXXXXXXXXX\n$ tailcat ssh tcXXXXXXXXX ls -la\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eMisc commands\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-misc-commands\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Misc commands\" href=\"#misc-commands\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003ePing to test connectivity; each pong reports whether it arrived via a\nDERP relay or a direct path. \u003ccode\u003e--until-direct\u003c/code\u003e keeps pinging (up to\n\u003ccode\u003e--timeout\u003c/code\u003e, default 10s) until a direct path works, exiting non-zero\nif one doesn't:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"$ tailcat ping --until-direct \u0026lt;token\u0026gt;\npong in 42.1ms via DERP(sfo)\npong in 1.2ms via 203.0.113.7:41641\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003e$ tailcat ping --until-direct \u003cspan class=\"pl-k\"\u003e\u0026lt;\u003c/span\u003etoken\u003cspan class=\"pl-k\"\u003e\u0026gt;\u003c/span\u003e\npong \u003cspan class=\"pl-k\"\u003ein\u003c/span\u003e 42.1ms via DERP(sfo)\npong \u003cspan class=\"pl-k\"\u003ein\u003c/span\u003e 1.2ms via 203.0.113.7:41641\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eRun a command through a SOCKS5 proxy routed over the tunnel:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"$ tailcat socks \u0026lt;token\u0026gt; curl http://server.tailcat:8081/\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003e$ tailcat socks \u003cspan class=\"pl-k\"\u003e\u0026lt;\u003c/span\u003etoken\u003cspan class=\"pl-k\"\u003e\u0026gt;\u003c/span\u003e curl http://server.tailcat:8081/\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eTokens also work directly as URL hostnames: the SOCKS proxy recognizes\nand dials them, so the token argument is optional. (Tokens are\ncase-sensitive; this works with curl and most CLI tools, but not with\nbrowsers, which lowercase hostnames.)\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"$ tailcat socks curl http://\u0026lt;token\u0026gt;:8081/\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003e$ tailcat socks curl http://\u003cspan class=\"pl-k\"\u003e\u0026lt;\u003c/span\u003etoken\u003cspan class=\"pl-k\"\u003e\u0026gt;\u003c/span\u003e:8081/\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eAct as an exit node so the client can reach the server's network:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"$ tailcat --serve=exit-node\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003e$ tailcat --serve=exit-node\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eParse a connection token and print its contents (the server's WireGuard\npublic key and DERP info) as JSON, without connecting to anything:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"$ tailcat parse tcomFwWCCcjS5nKNqAod034nWoJZW0LZqDhhC8U_dKdnDRYQ8uNGFpGQEu\n{\n \u0026quot;ServerPublic\u0026quot;: \u0026quot;nodekey:9c8d2e6728da80a1dd37e275a82595b42d9a838610bc53f74a7670d1610f2e34\u0026quot;,\n \u0026quot;RegionID\u0026quot;: 302\n}\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003e$ tailcat parse tcomFwWCCcjS5nKNqAod034nWoJZW0LZqDhhC8U_dKdnDRYQ8uNGFpGQEu\n{\n \u003cspan class=\"pl-s\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003eServerPublic\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e: \u003cspan class=\"pl-s\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003enodekey:9c8d2e6728da80a1dd37e275a82595b42d9a838610bc53f74a7670d1610f2e34\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e,\n \u003cspan class=\"pl-s\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003eRegionID\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e: 302\n}\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eResolve a short token (which references a DERP region by ID, requiring\nclients to fetch the DERP map) into a longer self-contained one with the\nDERP server info embedded, letting clients connect more quickly:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"$ tailcat resolve tcomFwWCCcjS5nKNqAod034nWoJZW0LZqDhhC8U_dKdnDRYQ8uNGFpGQEu\ntcomFwWCCcjS5nKNqAod034nWoJZW0LZqDhhC8U_dKdnDRYQ8uNGFygaFhToGjYWhudGMzMDJhLmlwbi5kZXZhNG0yMDguMTExLjM5LjM4YTZzMjYwNzpmNzQwOjA6M2Y6OjcyMA\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003e$ tailcat resolve tcomFwWCCcjS5nKNqAod034nWoJZW0LZqDhhC8U_dKdnDRYQ8uNGFpGQEu\ntcomFwWCCcjS5nKNqAod034nWoJZW0LZqDhhC8U_dKdnDRYQ8uNGFygaFhToGjYWhudGMzMDJhLmlwbi5kZXZhNG0yMDguMTExLjM5LjM4YTZzMjYwNzpmNzQwOjA6M2Y6OjcyMA\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eParsing that resolved token shows the embedded DERP info:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"$ tailcat parse tcomFwWCCcjS5nKNqAod034nWoJZW0LZqDhhC8U_dKdnDRYQ8uNGFygaFhToGjYWhudGMzMDJhLmlwbi5kZXZhNG0yMDguMTExLjM5LjM4YTZzMjYwNzpmNzQwOjA6M2Y6OjcyMA\n{\n \u0026quot;ServerPublic\u0026quot;: \u0026quot;nodekey:9c8d2e6728da80a1dd37e275a82595b42d9a838610bc53f74a7670d1610f2e34\u0026quot;,\n \u0026quot;Region\u0026quot;: [\n {\n \u0026quot;Nodes\u0026quot;: [\n {\n \u0026quot;HostName\u0026quot;: \u0026quot;tc302a.ipn.dev\u0026quot;,\n \u0026quot;IPv4\u0026quot;: \u0026quot;208.111.39.38\u0026quot;,\n \u0026quot;IPv6\u0026quot;: \u0026quot;2607:f740:0:3f::720\u0026quot;\n }\n ]\n }\n ]\n}\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003e$ tailcat parse tcomFwWCCcjS5nKNqAod034nWoJZW0LZqDhhC8U_dKdnDRYQ8uNGFygaFhToGjYWhudGMzMDJhLmlwbi5kZXZhNG0yMDguMTExLjM5LjM4YTZzMjYwNzpmNzQwOjA6M2Y6OjcyMA\n{\n \u003cspan class=\"pl-s\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003eServerPublic\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e: \u003cspan class=\"pl-s\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003enodekey:9c8d2e6728da80a1dd37e275a82595b42d9a838610bc53f74a7670d1610f2e34\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e,\n \u003cspan class=\"pl-s\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003eRegion\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e: [\n {\n \u003cspan class=\"pl-s\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003eNodes\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e: [\n {\n \u003cspan class=\"pl-s\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003eHostName\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e: \u003cspan class=\"pl-s\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003etc302a.ipn.dev\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e,\n \u003cspan class=\"pl-s\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003eIPv4\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e: \u003cspan class=\"pl-s\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003e208.111.39.38\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e,\n \u003cspan class=\"pl-s\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003eIPv6\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e: \u003cspan class=\"pl-s\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003e2607:f740:0:3f::720\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\n }\n ]\n }\n ]\n}\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eA server can print the long self-contained form directly with the\n\u003ccode\u003e--full-address\u003c/code\u003e flag.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eKey Management\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-key-management\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Key Management\" href=\"#key-management\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eA server's address (connection token) is derived from its WireGuard key, so\nthe key you use determines who can reach you:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eEphemeral keys (the default):\u003c/strong\u003e each server run generates a fresh key in\nmemory and prints an address nobody has ever seen. When the process exits,\nthe key is discarded and the address is dead forever. This is the safe\ndefault: sharing that address only ever refers to that one run.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eSaved keys:\u003c/strong\u003e \u003ccode\u003etailcat genkey\u003c/code\u003e generates a key saved to disk so the\naddress stays stable across restarts. The flip side: anyone you've \u003cem\u003eever\u003c/em\u003e\nshared that address with can connect to any future server using that key,\nunless you restrict clients with \u003ccode\u003e--allow\u003c/code\u003e (see \u003ccode\u003etailcat genkey --client\u003c/code\u003e).\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe CLI says at startup which kind it's using, so you know whether you're\nstarting a fresh single-use server or re-listening on an address you may\nhave shared in the past.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"$ tailcat genkey --region=nyc\n# prints the token; key saved to ~/.config/tailcat/keys/default.private.json\n\n# later; the key named \u0026quot;default\u0026quot; is used automatically once it exists:\n$ tailcat --serve=8080\n# 🐈 Server listening with saved key \u0026quot;default\u0026quot;: tcXXXXXXXXX\n\n# ... unless you force a one-off ephemeral key:\n$ tailcat --serve=8080 --key=new\n# 🐈 Server listening with new address: tcXXXXXXXXX\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003e$ tailcat genkey --region=nyc\n\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e#\u003c/span\u003e prints the token; key saved to ~/.config/tailcat/keys/default.private.json\u003c/span\u003e\n\n\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e#\u003c/span\u003e later; the key named \"default\" is used automatically once it exists:\u003c/span\u003e\n$ tailcat --serve=8080\n\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e#\u003c/span\u003e 🐈 Server listening with saved key \"default\": tcXXXXXXXXX\u003c/span\u003e\n\n\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e#\u003c/span\u003e ... unless you force a one-off ephemeral key:\u003c/span\u003e\n$ tailcat --serve=8080 --key=new\n\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e#\u003c/span\u003e 🐈 Server listening with new address: tcXXXXXXXXX\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThat is, \u003ccode\u003edefault\u003c/code\u003e is a magic key name: once it exists, plain \u003ccode\u003etailcat\u003c/code\u003e\nsilently uses it instead of generating an ephemeral key, and the startup\nline above is what tells you which happened. Use \u003ccode\u003e--key=new\u003c/code\u003e to get an\nephemeral key anyway, \u003ccode\u003e--key=\u0026lt;name\u0026gt;\u003c/code\u003e to use a different saved key, or\n\u003ccode\u003etailcat genkey --delete --key=default\u003c/code\u003e to remove the saved default key.\n\u003ccode\u003etailcat genkey --list\u003c/code\u003e lists your saved keys.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eTokens can also be published as DNS TXT records and looked up by name;\na DNS name works anywhere the CLI takes a token:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"# If example.com has a TXT record \u0026quot;tailcat=tc...\u0026quot;\n$ tailcat example.com 8080\n$ tailcat ssh example.com\n$ tailcat ping example.com\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e#\u003c/span\u003e If example.com has a TXT record \"tailcat=tc...\"\u003c/span\u003e\n$ tailcat example.com 8080\n$ tailcat ssh example.com\n$ tailcat ping example.com\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eExamples\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-examples\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Examples\" href=\"#examples\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eProtected SSH server over DNS\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-protected-ssh-server-over-dns\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Protected SSH server over DNS\" href=\"#protected-ssh-server-over-dns\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eWho needs port forwarding or port knocking? This runs an SSH server\nreachable from anywhere by name, with no open inbound ports on the\nserver, where WireGuard authenticates the client before the SSH\nserver ever sees a packet.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eOn the client machine, generate a client identity keypair. It prints\nthe public key, which is all the server needs to know:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"client$ tailcat genkey --client\n# wrote file to ~/.config/tailcat/keys/client-default.private.json\nnodekey:cfb6bfa77a0654d7450947fd6acef17d2cd848da1d30b2540b13dac272ddfd16\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003eclient$ tailcat genkey --client\n\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e#\u003c/span\u003e wrote file to ~/.config/tailcat/keys/client-default.private.json\u003c/span\u003e\nnodekey:cfb6bfa77a0654d7450947fd6acef17d2cd848da1d30b2540b13dac272ddfd16\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eOn the server, generate a server keypair pinned to its nearest DERP\nregion (see why below), then serve SSH to only that client:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"server$ tailcat genkey --fixed-region\n# wrote file to ~/.config/tailcat/keys/default.private.json\ntcXXXXXXXXX\n\nserver$ tailcat --serve=22 --allow=nodekey:cfb6bf...ddfd16\n# 🐈 Server listening with saved key \u0026quot;default\u0026quot;: tcXXXXXXXXX\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003eserver$ tailcat genkey --fixed-region\n\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e#\u003c/span\u003e wrote file to ~/.config/tailcat/keys/default.private.json\u003c/span\u003e\ntcXXXXXXXXX\n\nserver$ tailcat --serve=22 --allow=nodekey:cfb6bf...ddfd16\n\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c\"\u003e#\u003c/span\u003e 🐈 Server listening with saved key \"default\": tcXXXXXXXXX\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003ePublish the token in DNS as a TXT record:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"my-server.example.com. 300 IN TXT \u0026quot;tailcat=tcXXXXXXXXX\u0026quot;\"\u003e\u003cpre class=\"notranslate\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003emy-server.example.com. 300 IN TXT \"tailcat=tcXXXXXXXXX\"\n\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eAnd then the client side is just:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"client$ tailcat ssh my-server.example.com\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003eclient$ tailcat ssh my-server.example.com\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eClient modes automatically use the saved \u003ccode\u003eclient-default\u003c/code\u003e key when it\nexists, so no extra flags are needed to present the allowed identity.\nAnyone else's handshake is silently ignored: they can't reach the SSH\nserver, or even learn that one is running.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eWhy \u003ccode\u003e--fixed-region\u003c/code\u003e: it discovers the nearest DERP region once, at\ngenkey time, and bakes its ID into both the printed token and the\nsaved key file, so server restarts bind to the same region (keeping\nthe published token valid) without re-probing. Plain \u003ccode\u003etailcat genkey\u003c/code\u003e\ndefaults to \u003ccode\u003e--region=auto\u003c/code\u003e, which instead bakes in \"pick at\nstartup\": fine for one-off use, but a token published in DNS should\nname a fixed region so clients and future server restarts all\nrendezvous in the same place. (\u003ccode\u003e--region=\u0026lt;name\u0026gt;\u003c/code\u003e pins an explicit one\ninstead; \u003ccode\u003e--region=list\u003c/code\u003e shows the choices.)\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eTODO: make the client more robust here if the DERP map changes over\ntime: \u003ca class=\"issue-link js-issue-link\" data-error-text=\"Failed to load title\" data-id=\"5258120127\" data-permission-text=\"Title is private\" data-url=\"https://github.com/tailscale/tailcat/issues/7\" data-hovercard-type=\"issue\" data-hovercard-url=\"/tailscale/tailcat/issues/7/hovercard\" href=\"https://github.com/tailscale/tailcat/issues/7\"\u003e#7\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eBring your own DERP relay\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-bring-your-own-derp-relay\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Bring your own DERP relay\" href=\"#bring-your-own-derp-relay\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eNothing requires Tailscale's relays: \u003ca href=\"https://github.com/tailscale/tailscale/tree/main/cmd/derper#derp\"\u003erun your own DERP\nserver\u003c/a\u003e\n(it needs a hostname with a TLS certificate, which derper can get\nitself via Let's Encrypt), then generate a server key that uses it by\npassing its hostname (or several, comma-separated) as the region:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"server$ tailcat genkey --region=derp.example.com\ntcomFwWCCAIsKOqPUux6ClG2RM4A_vOq4VBzGgHGGjq9OsJuFKSWFygaFhToGhYWhwZGVycC5leGFtcGxlLmNvbQ\n\nserver$ tailcat --serve=22\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003eserver$ tailcat genkey --region=derp.example.com\ntcomFwWCCAIsKOqPUux6ClG2RM4A_vOq4VBzGgHGGjq9OsJuFKSWFygaFhToGhYWhwZGVycC5leGFtcGxlLmNvbQ\n\nserver$ tailcat --serve=22\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe token embeds your relay's hostname:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"$ tailcat parse tcomFwWCCAIsKOqPUux6ClG2RM4A_vOq4VBzGgHGGjq9OsJuFKSWFygaFhToGhYWhwZGVycC5leGFtcGxlLmNvbQ\n{\n \u0026quot;ServerPublic\u0026quot;: \u0026quot;nodekey:8022c28ea8f52ec7a0a51b644ce00fef3aae150731a01c61a3abd3ac26e14a49\u0026quot;,\n \u0026quot;Region\u0026quot;: [\n {\n \u0026quot;Nodes\u0026quot;: [\n {\n \u0026quot;HostName\u0026quot;: \u0026quot;derp.example.com\u0026quot;\n }\n ]\n }\n ]\n}\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003e$ tailcat parse tcomFwWCCAIsKOqPUux6ClG2RM4A_vOq4VBzGgHGGjq9OsJuFKSWFygaFhToGhYWhwZGVycC5leGFtcGxlLmNvbQ\n{\n \u003cspan class=\"pl-s\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003eServerPublic\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e: \u003cspan class=\"pl-s\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003enodekey:8022c28ea8f52ec7a0a51b644ce00fef3aae150731a01c61a3abd3ac26e14a49\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e,\n \u003cspan class=\"pl-s\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003eRegion\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e: [\n {\n \u003cspan class=\"pl-s\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003eNodes\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e: [\n {\n \u003cspan class=\"pl-s\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003eHostName\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e: \u003cspan class=\"pl-s\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003ederp.example.com\u003cspan class=\"pl-pds\"\u003e\"\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\n }\n ]\n }\n ]\n}\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eso clients need no extra flags and never contact Tailscale's DERP map\nserver or relays, and the only rate limits are yours. Alternatively,\nif you run a whole fleet of relays, serve your own DERP map JSON and\npoint both sides at it with \u003ccode\u003e--derpmap-url\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eGo library\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-go-library\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Go library\" href=\"#go-library\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eA minimal server that answers any TCP port through the tunnel and\nprints its token. The zero value Server picks defaults for anything\nunset: a fresh ephemeral key, the nearest region of the default DERP\nmap, and \u003ccode\u003elog.Printf\u003c/code\u003e logging (set \u003ccode\u003eLogf\u003c/code\u003e to \u003ccode\u003elogger.Discard\u003c/code\u003e for\nquiet):\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-go notranslate position-relative port)\n\t\t\t\tc.Close()\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t},\n\t}\n\tif err := s.Start(); err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Fatal(err)\n\t}\n\tfmt.Println(s.ConnBlob())\n\tselect {}\n}\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-c1\"\u003eConnBlob\u003c/span\u003e())\n\t\u003cspan class=\"pl-k\"\u003eselect\u003c/span\u003e {}\n}\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eAnd a minimal client that dials it, given that token as its argument.\nLike Server, the Client zero value works with just its Server token\nfield set (\u003ccode\u003etailcat.NewClient\u003c/code\u003e is shorthand for exactly that), and\nthe tunnel is established lazily by the first dial:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-go notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\"
